\langle\pi_{\gamma\alpha}(q):\alpha\in x\cap\gamma\rangle\in\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction(x\cap\gamma),\vec{\pi}\restriction(x\cap\gamma))
, and for all
\beta\in x
, the maps down to
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in x\cap\beta
commute below
\pi_{\gamma\beta}(q)
. So below
\langle\pi_{\gamma\alpha}(q):\alpha\in x\cap\gamma\rangle
,
\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction(x\cap\gamma),\vec{\pi}\restriction(x\cap\gamma)\rangle
is a
\kappa
-good inverse system. Condition (
4
) requires that, below
q
,
\mathbb{Q}_{\gamma}
projects to the inverse limit in the natural way.

We will use the flexibility in choosing the conditions
q_{\beta\alpha}
to obtain a strategic closure. This will play a crucial role in the proof of Lemma
53
, in which we use the
\mathbb{U}
forcing, which is defined in a smaller forcing extension, as a termspace forcing for
\mathbb{U}
as defined in a larger extension. In this case, the commutativity of the projections and the existence of inverse limits might occur only below a sufficiently strong condition.

Let
U\subseteq\mathbb{U}(G)
be generic and let
\vec{\mathbb{Q}}_{U}=\bigcup_{u\in U}\vec{\mathbb{Q}^{u}}
.
We say that a condition
\langle\mathbb{Q},1,\sigma\rangle
appears in
U
if it belongs to the range of
\vec{\mathbb{Q}}_{U}
.

Lemma 39
.

If
G*U\subseteq\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
is generic, then for all
\langle\mathbb{P},p,\sigma\rangle\in G
, there is a coding condition
\langle\mathbb{Q},1,\tau\rangle
appearing in
U
such that for some
q\in G^{\tau}_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
\langle\mathbb{Q},q,\tau\rangle\leq\langle\mathbb{P},p,\sigma\rangle
.

Proof.

Suppose
G\subseteq\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
is generic. Let
u=\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u},\vec{\pi}^{u},\vec{q}^{u}\rangle\in\mathbb{U}(G)
and
\langle\mathbb{P},p,\sigma\rangle\in G
be arbitrary. Suppose
\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u}
has length
\delta+1
, and let
\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\delta},1_{\delta},\tau_{\delta}\rangle
be the top element of
\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u}
. Let
\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\delta+1},q^{*},\tau_{\delta+1}\rangle\in G
be a coding condition below both
\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\delta},1_{\delta},\tau_{\delta}\rangle
and
\langle\mathbb{P},p,\sigma\rangle
. Let
\rho
be a projection witnessing
\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\delta+1},q^{*},\tau_{\delta+1}\rangle\leq\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\delta},1_{\delta},\tau_{\delta}\rangle
.
Let
\pi_{\delta+1,\delta}^{u^{\prime}}=\rho
, and for
\alpha<\delta
, let
\pi_{\delta+1,\alpha}^{u^{\prime}}=\pi_{\delta\alpha}^{u}\circ\rho
.

Let
q^{u^{\prime}}_{\delta+1,\delta}=q^{*}
, and for
\alpha<\delta
, let
q^{u^{\prime}}_{\delta+1,\alpha}
be some
r\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\tau}
below
q^{*}
such that
\rho(r)\leq q^{u}_{\delta\alpha}
.
Let
u^{\prime}
be the associated extensions of
u
, with the
\delta+1^{st}
element of
\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u^{\prime}}
being
\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\delta+1},1_{\mathbb{Q}_{\delta+1}},\tau_{\delta+1}\rangle
. It suffices to show that
u^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(G)
.

Let us verify condition (
4
). Suppose
x\in[\delta+1]^{<\kappa}
, and let
x^{\prime}=x\cup\{\delta+1\}
.
Suppose
q\in\mathbb{Q}_{\delta+1}
and
\pi_{\delta+1,\beta}(q)\leq q^{u^{\prime}}_{\beta\alpha}
for all
\{\alpha,\beta\}\in[x^{\prime}]^{2}
. By the definition of
\vec{\pi}^{u^{\prime}}
and
\vec{q}^{u^{\prime}}
,
\rho(q)
and
\pi_{\delta\alpha}(\rho(q))
are defined for each
\alpha\in x\cap\delta
, and
\pi^{u}_{\delta\beta}(\rho(q))\leq q^{u}_{\beta\alpha}
for all
\{\alpha,\beta\}\in[x\cup\{\delta\}]^{2}
.

Suppose
\vec{r}\in\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u^{\prime}}\restriction x,\vec{\pi}^{u^{\prime}}\restriction x)
and
\vec{r}\leq\langle\pi_{\delta+1,\alpha}(q):\alpha\in x\rangle
. If
\delta\in x
, put
q^{\prime}=\vec{r}(\delta)
, and otherwise use the assumption that
u\in\mathbb{U}(G)
to find
q^{\prime}\leq\rho(q)
in
\mathbb{Q}_{\delta}
such that
\langle\pi_{\delta\alpha}(q^{\prime}):\alpha\in x\rangle\leq\vec{r}\restriction\delta
. Then find
q^{\prime\prime}\leq q
in
E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u^{\prime}},\vec{\pi}^{u^{\prime}}\rangle}_{\delta+1}
such that
\rho(q^{\prime\prime})\leq q^{\prime}
,
so that
\langle\pi_{\delta+1,\alpha}(q^{\prime\prime}):\alpha\in x\rangle\leq\vec{r}
.
∎

Lemma 40
.

\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
is strongly
\kappa
-strategically closed.

Proof.

Let us play the game
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{I}}_{\kappa}(\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G}))
. We will use the quite reasonable
(\kappa,\lambda)
-collapse
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)
to guide our strategy for Player II. Later, we will refer to the strategy we describe as
\Sigma
and invoke some of its specific properties.

Let us denote the conditions in the play as
\langle p_{i},\dot{u}_{i}\rangle
, where
p_{i}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{i},r_{i},\tau_{i}\rangle
.

Suppose Player I plays
\langle\langle\mathbb{P}_{0},r_{0},\tau_{0}\rangle,\dot{u}_{0}\rangle
. By extending the first coordinate if necessary, we may assume it decides the top
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
-condition in
\dot{u}_{0}
to be some
\langle\mathbb{Q},1,\sigma\rangle
, and then since this is compatible with
\langle\mathbb{P}_{0},r_{0},\tau_{0}\rangle
, we can find a common coding extension
\langle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\prime},r_{0}^{\prime},\tau_{0}^{\prime}\rangle
. Then, using the proof of Lemma
39
, we can extend
\dot{u}_{0}
to
\dot{u}_{0}^{\prime}
, such that
\langle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\prime},r_{0}^{\prime},\tau_{0}^{\prime}\rangle
forces
\langle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\prime},1,\tau_{0}^{\prime}\rangle
to be at the top of
\dot{u}_{0}^{\prime}
.
Let
\nu
be a cardinal such that
|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\prime}|<\nu<\lambda
and
\nu^{<\kappa}=\nu
, and let
\alpha_{1}=\nu+1
. By Lemma
11
, both
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\prime}\times\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,\nu)
and
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{1})
have
\kappa
-closed dense sets isomorphic to
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,\nu)
, so let
\rho
be a
\kappa
-continuous projection from a dense
\kappa
-closed subset of
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{1})
to
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\prime}
.
Let
r_{1}
and
\tau_{1}
be such that
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{1}),r_{1},\tau_{1}\rangle\leq\langle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\prime},r_{0}^{\prime},\tau_{0}^{\prime}\rangle
, with the former also a coding condition, and with the ordering witnessed by
\rho
.
Again appealing to Lemma
39
, let
\dot{u}_{1}
be a one-point extension of
\dot{u}_{0}^{\prime}
with
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{1}),1,\tau_{1}\rangle
at the top. We set the projections of
\dot{u}_{1}
to be the composition of the projection
\rho
with the previous projections
\vec{\pi}^{\dot{u}_{0}^{\prime}}
. Letting
\dot{\delta}+1
be the length of the condition
\dot{u}_{0}^{\prime}
, we extend its sequence
\vec{q}
by taking
q^{\dot{u}_{1}}_{\dot{\delta}+1,\alpha}
, for
\alpha<\dot{\delta}
, to be a name for a condition in
G^{\tau_{1}}_{\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{1})}
below
r_{1}
projected by
\rho
below
q^{\dot{u}_{0}^{\prime}}_{\dot{\delta},\alpha}
, and we set
q^{\dot{u}_{1}}_{\dot{\delta}+1,\dot{\delta}}=r_{1}
.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{1}=\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{1})
, and let
\langle\langle\mathbb{P}_{1},r_{1},\tau_{1}\rangle,\dot{u}_{1}\rangle
be Player II’s first move.

Suppose Player I responds with
\langle\langle\mathbb{P}_{2},r_{2},\tau_{2}\rangle,\dot{u}_{2}\rangle
. We first strengthen it as above, so that we can assume
\langle\mathbb{P}_{2},r_{2},\tau_{2}\rangle
forces
\langle\mathbb{P}_{2},1,\tau_{2}\rangle
to be at the top of
\dot{u}_{2}
, and also so that
\langle\mathbb{P}_{2},q_{2},\tau_{2}\rangle
decides the projection that
\dot{u}_{2}
assigns from
\mathbb{P}_{2}
to
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{1})
, say as
\pi^{*}
. Then appealing to Lemma
25
and Remark
27
, we can find
\alpha_{3}
,
r_{3}
, and
\tau_{3}
such that
r_{3}\leq r_{1}
in
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)
,
1\Vdash\tau_{3}\trianglerighteq\tau_{1}
,
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{3}),r_{3},\tau_{3}\rangle
is a coding condition below
\langle\mathbb{P}_{2},r_{2},\tau_{2}\rangle
, say with witnessing projection
\rho
,
and on a
\kappa
-closed dense set,
\pi^{*}\circ\rho
equals the restriction map
p\mapsto p\restriction\alpha_{1}
. Note that here we ask this commutativity to hold on a dense set and not just below a certain condition.

Let
\dot{u}_{3}
be a name for a one-point extension of
\dot{u}_{2}
with
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{3}),1,\tau_{3}\rangle
at the top, but with the projections chosen in the following way:

•

The projection from
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{3})
to
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{1})
is the restriction map,
r\mapsto r\restriction\alpha_{1}
.

•

The projection from
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{3})
to posets
\mathbb{Q}^{\dot{u}_{2}}_{\zeta}
, appearing in
\dot{u}_{2}
between
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{1})
and
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{3})
is, as in Lemma
39
, the composition
\pi\circ\rho
, where
\pi
is the projection that
\dot{u}_{2}
chooses from
\mathbb{P}_{2}
to
\mathbb{Q}^{\dot{u}_{2}}_{\zeta}
.

•

For each
\zeta
smaller than the index of
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{3})
, the projection from
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{3})
to
\mathbb{Q}^{\dot{u}_{2}}_{\zeta}
is the composition
\pi\circ(\cdot\restriction\alpha_{1})
where
\pi
is the projection that
\dot{u}_{2}
chooses from
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{1})
to
\mathbb{Q}^{\dot{u}_{2}}_{\zeta}
.

The sequence of conditions
\vec{q}^{u_{2}}
is extended as follows. Working in an extension by
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
obtained by forcing below
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{3}),r_{3},\tau_{3}\rangle
, suppose the posets played by Player I occupy places
\xi_{0}<\xi_{1}
in
u_{2}
, so that those played by II will occupy places
\xi_{0}+1
and
\xi_{1}+1
in
u_{3}
.
The conditions
q^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{1}+1,\alpha}
for
\alpha\leq\xi_{1}
are chosen from
G^{\tau_{3}}_{\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{3})}
with the following properties:

•

q^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{1}+1,\xi_{0}+1}=1
.

•

For
\alpha\leq\xi_{0}
,
q^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{1}+1,\alpha}\restriction\alpha_{1}\leq q^{u_{2}}_{\xi_{0}+1,\alpha}
.

•

For
\xi_{0}+1<\alpha\leq\xi_{1}
,
q^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{1}+1,\alpha}\leq r_{3}
and
\rho(q^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{1}+1,\alpha})\leq q^{u_{2}}_{\xi_{1},\alpha}.

To check clause (
4
), suppose
x\in[\xi_{1}+1]^{<\kappa}
,
q\in\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{3})
is such that
\pi_{\xi_{1}+1,\beta}(q)\leq q^{u_{3}}_{\beta\alpha}
for
\{\alpha,\beta\}\in[x\cup\{\xi_{1}+1\}]^{2}
. Suppose
\vec{r}\leq\langle\pi_{\xi_{1}+1,\alpha}(q):\alpha\in x\rangle
in
\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u_{3}}\restriction x,\vec{\pi}^{u_{3}}\restriction x)
. If
\sup(x)>\xi_{0}
, then we find the desired
q\leq q^{\prime}
as in the proof of Lemma
39
. If
\sup(x)\leq\xi_{0}
, then there is
q^{\prime}\leq q\restriction\alpha_{1}
with the desired property by induction, and we can take
q^{\prime\prime}=q\cup q^{\prime}
.

Let us assume inductively that
\delta<\kappa
and the game has gone on for
\delta
rounds, and Player II’s moves satisfy the following for odd
i<\delta
:

•

Each of II’s moves is of the form
\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{i}),r_{i},\tau_{i}\rangle,\dot{u}_{i}\rangle
, where

\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{i}),1,\tau_{i}\rangle
is forced to be at the top of
\dot{u}_{i}
.

•

For odd
j<i
,
r_{i}\restriction\alpha_{j}\supseteq r_{j}
,
1\Vdash\tau_{i}\trianglerighteq\tau_{j}
,
it is forced that the projection chosen by
\dot{u}_{i}
from
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{i})
to
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{j})
is the restriction map,
r\mapsto r\restriction\alpha_{j}
, and the condition
q_{\beta_{i},\beta_{j}}\in\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{i})
, where
\beta_{j}<\beta_{i}
are the indices of these posets in
u_{i}
, is just the trivial condition.

•

It is forced that for any
\mathbb{Q}
appearing in
\dot{u}_{i}
, if
j
is such that
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{j})
appears after
\mathbb{Q}
, and it is the poset in the first coordinate of a previous play by II, and the indices in
\dot{u}_{i}
of
\mathbb{Q},\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{j}),\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{i})
are
\gamma,\beta_{j},\beta_{i}
respectively, then
\pi^{u_{i}}_{\beta_{i},\gamma}=\pi^{u_{i}}_{\beta_{j},\gamma}\circ(\cdot\restriction\alpha_{j})
, restricted to some
\kappa
-closed dense set.
Furthermore, it is forced that
q_{\beta_{i},\gamma}\restriction\alpha_{j}\leq q_{\beta_{j},\gamma}
.

We must show two things: first, that a lower bound at stage
\delta
exists when
\delta
is a limit, and second, that Player II can continue to play moves that satisfy these assumptions. If
\delta
is not a limit, Player II responds just as in round 3.

Suppose
\delta
is a limit ordinal.
Let us first describe the
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
-condition. Let
\alpha_{\delta}=\sup\{\alpha_{i}:i<\delta
is odd
\}
. Let
r_{\delta}=\bigcup\{r_{i}:i<\delta
is odd
\}
. Let
\tau_{\delta}
be a
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta})
-name for the union of
\{\tau_{i}:i<\delta
is odd
\}
(formally, pulled to be
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta})
-names). By the remarks just after Lemma
30
,
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta}),r_{\delta},\tau_{\delta}\rangle
is a coding condition, and it is a lower bound to the first coordinates of the previously played conditions.

To define
\dot{u}_{\delta}
, temporarily work in an extension
V[G]
by
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
obtained by forcing below
p_{\delta}=\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta}),r_{\delta},\tau_{\delta}\rangle
. Suppose
\langle\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\beta},1,\sigma_{\beta}\rangle:\beta<\xi\rangle
,
\langle\pi_{\beta\beta^{\prime}},q_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}:\beta^{\prime}<\beta<\xi\rangle

are the sequences given by amalgamating the
\dot{u}_{i}^{G}
for
i<\delta
.
For an odd ordinal
i<\delta
, let
\beta_{i}
be the index below
\xi
of the poset
\mathbb{P}_{i}=\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{i})
played by II.

Let us define the condition
u_{\delta}
, extending
u_{i}
for all
i<\delta
.
Let
\langle\mathbb{Q}^{u_{\delta}}_{\xi},1,\sigma_{\xi}\rangle=\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta}),1,\tau_{\delta}\rangle
.
To define the projections, first if
\gamma=\beta_{i}
for some odd

, let
\pi^{u_{\delta}}_{\xi\gamma}=\cdot\restriction\alpha_{i}
, and let

It is easy to check that
D_{\gamma}
is a
\kappa
-closed dense subset of
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta})

Define

is such that

. (By the induction hypothesis, it does not matter which such

To define

, let

be a condition

in the generic filter of
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta})
, such that whenever
\beta_{j}<\gamma<\beta_{i}
,

, and
\pi_{\xi\gamma}(q)\leq q_{\gamma,\beta_{j}}
.
As those are
{<}\kappa
-many conditions below which to project, such a condition

exists. We have that for all
\gamma<\xi
,
\pi^{u_{\delta}}_{\xi\gamma}
witnesses the ordering

\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta}),q_{\xi\gamma}^{u_{\delta}},\tau_{\delta}\rangle\leq\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\gamma},1,\sigma_{\gamma}\rangle
.

Working in
V[G]
, let us show that
u_{\delta}
satisfies condition (
4
). Let
x\in[\xi]^{<\kappa}
, and let

. Let

. Note that if

whenever

.
Furthermore, if

.
It follows that for all

\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\beta},\pi_{\xi\beta}(q),\sigma_{\beta}\rangle\leq\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha},1,\sigma_{\alpha}\rangle

\mathbb{U}(G)
, the system of maps

Suppose
q\in E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u_{\delta}}\restriction x^{\prime},\vec{\pi}^{u_{\delta}}\restriction x^{\prime}\rangle}

\xi
, let

be such that
\beta_{i}>\sup x
. Then

is below

u_{i}
is a condition and the projections commute below

, there is

such that
\langle\pi_{\beta_{i},\gamma}(q^{\prime}):\gamma\in x\rangle\leq\vec{r}
. Then let
q^{\prime\prime}=q\cup q^{\prime}
.

If
x
is cofinal in
\xi
, then by the above observations,

q\in E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u_{\delta}}\restriction(x^{\prime}\cup y),\vec{\pi}^{u_{\delta}}\restriction(x^{\prime}\cup y)\rangle}.

By Lemma
18
, below
\langle\pi_{\xi\gamma}(q):\gamma\in x\cup y\rangle
,

\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u_{\delta}}\restriction(x\cup y),\vec{\pi}^{u_{\delta}}\restriction(x\cup y))

is canonically isomorphic to a dense subset of

\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u_{\delta}}\restriction x,\vec{\pi}^{u_{\delta}}\restriction x)
via the restriction map. Thus we can find
\vec{s}\in\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u_{\delta}}\restriction(x\cup y),\vec{\pi}^{u_{\delta}}\restriction(x\cup y))
such that
\vec{s}\restriction x\leq\vec{r}
, and take
q^{\prime}\leq q
as
\bigcup_{i<\delta}\vec{s}(\beta_{i})
, witnessing (
4
).

So, at the
\delta^{th}
step, Player I has a possible move. Let us show that Player II can continue similarly after a limit-stage move of Player I,
\langle p_{\delta},\dot{u}_{\delta}\rangle
, where
p_{\delta}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\delta},r_{\delta},\tau_{\delta}\rangle
.
The main idea is that Player II can play almost as if Player I had played the lower bound constructed above.

Working temporarily in an extension
V[G]
by
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
obtained by forcing below
p_{\delta}
, assume
\dot{u}_{\delta}^{G}
takes the form
\langle\langle\langle\mathbb{Q}_{i},1,\sigma_{i}\rangle:i\leq\xi\rangle,\langle\pi_{ji}:i<j\leq\xi\rangle,\langle q_{ji}:i<j\leq\xi\rangle\rangle
.
Let
\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{i}),r_{i},\tau_{i}\rangle,\dot{u}_{i}\rangle
be Player II’s moves for odd
i<\delta
, and let
\beta_{i}
be the index in
u_{\delta}
where
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{i})
appears. Let
\alpha_{\delta}=\sup_{i}\alpha_{i}
,
\beta_{\delta}=\sup_{i}\beta_{i}
, and
y=\{\beta_{i}:i<\delta
is odd
\}
.
By strengthening

p_{\delta}
if necessary, we may assume that it decides the values of the ordinals
\xi,\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}
, the projections
\pi_{ji}
for
\{i,j\}\in[y\cup\{\xi\}]^{2}
, and that
|\mathbb{P}_{\delta}|=\nu>\alpha_{\delta}
, and
\mathbb{P}_{\delta}
collapses
\nu
to
\kappa
. As in Lemma
39
, we may also assume
that
\langle\mathbb{P}_{\delta},1,\tau_{\delta}\rangle
is forced to be at the top of
\dot{u}_{\delta}
at position
\xi
.

Since
u_{\delta}
satisfies condition (
4
), there will be
q^{*}
in the generic for
\mathbb{Q}_{\xi}=\mathbb{P}_{\delta}
such that the maps in the system
\langle\pi_{ji}:\{i,j\}\in[y\cup\{\xi\}]^{2}\rangle
commute below
q^{*}
, and
the natural map from
\mathbb{Q}_{\xi}
to
\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction y,\vec{\pi}\restriction y)
is a projection. Note that this inverse limit is canonically isomorphic to
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta})
. Using Theorem
11
, and strengthening
q^{*}
if necessary, we can construct a projection
\varphi\colon\mathbb{Q}_{\xi}\to\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta})
, defined on a
\kappa
-closed dense set, such that below
q^{*}
,
\varphi
agrees with the natural map to
\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction y,\vec{\pi}\restriction y)
.
10
10

10

This is done simply by selecting a dense tree as in Theorem
11
with
q^{*}
in the first level, noting the tree below any given node is isomorphic to the whole tree, noting that
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,<\nu+1)
also contains a dense copy of that tree, and gluing partial projections.
Without loss of generality,
q^{*}=r_{\delta}
.

If
\tau_{\delta}^{\prime}
is a name for the union of
\tau_{i}
for odd
i<\delta
, then we have that
\varphi
witnesses the ordering
\langle\mathbb{P}_{\delta},r_{\delta},\tau_{\delta}\rangle\leq\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta}),1,\tau_{\delta}^{\prime}\rangle
.
By Lemma
25
and Remark
27
, we can find a coding condition of the form
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta+1}),r_{\delta+1},\tau_{\delta+1}\rangle\leq\langle\mathbb{P}_{\delta},r_{\delta},\tau_{\delta}\rangle
with witnessing projection
\rho
such that
\varphi\circ\rho=\cdot\restriction\alpha_{\delta}
on some
\kappa
-closed dense set, and
\tau_{\delta+1}
is forced (by 1) to end-extend
\tau_{\delta}^{\prime}
.

We extend
\dot{u}_{\delta}
to
\dot{u}_{\delta+1}
that is forced to have
\mathbb{Q}^{u_{\delta+1}}_{\xi+1}=\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha_{\delta+1})
and
\sigma_{\xi+1}=\tau_{\delta+1}
.
For the projections,
\pi^{u_{\delta+1}}_{\xi+1,\gamma}
is defined as
\pi^{u_{\delta}}_{\xi\gamma}\circ\rho
for
\beta_{\delta}\leq\gamma\leq\xi
,
\pi^{u_{\delta+1}}_{\xi+1,\gamma}
is the restriction map when
\gamma\in y
, and for
\gamma\in\beta_{\delta}\setminus y
,

\pi^{u_{\delta+1}}_{\xi+1,\gamma}=\pi^{u_{\delta}}_{\beta_{i},\gamma}\circ(\cdot\restriction\beta_{i})
, where
\gamma<\beta_{i}<\beta_{\delta}
, restricted to the set of points
p
such that
\pi^{u_{\delta}}_{\gamma,\beta_{j}}\circ\pi^{u_{\delta}}_{\beta_{k},\gamma}(p\restriction\beta_{k})
is defined whenever
\beta_{j}<\gamma<\beta_{k}
.
For the conditions
q_{\xi+1,\gamma}
, we take

for
\gamma\in y
,

\rho(q)\leq q_{\xi,\gamma}^{u_{\delta}}
for
\beta_{\delta}\leq\gamma\leq\xi
,
and for
\gamma\in\beta_{\delta}\setminus y
, we take

to be some

in the generic below
r_{\delta+1}

such that whenever
\beta_{j}<\gamma<\beta_{i}
,

, and
\pi_{\xi\gamma}(q)\leq q_{\gamma,\beta_{j}}
.

Let us check that condition (

) holds for
\dot{u}_{\delta+1}
. Suppose
x\in[\xi+1]^{<\kappa}
and let
x^{\prime}=x\cup\{\xi+1\}
; we must show

is a projection from
E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction x^{\prime},\vec{\pi}\restriction x^{\prime}\rangle}_{\xi+1}
to
\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction x,\vec{\pi}\restriction x)
below the suitable condition. If
\sup x>\beta_{\delta}
, or

for some

, then we argue as in Lemma

. So suppose

,

\pi_{\xi+1,\beta}(q)\leq q_{\beta\alpha}
for all

. By construction,
q\in E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction(x^{\prime}\cup y),\vec{\pi}\restriction(x^{\prime}\cup y)\rangle}_{\xi+1}
, and the system of maps

commutes below

. If

∎

In the previous lemma, we used a reasonable collapse in order to guide our strategy for
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)\ast\dot{\mathbb{U}(G)}
. As the first component of this iteration is only
\kappa
-closed, we could not hope for a much stronger closure than what we got. In the next lemma, we consider a case in which the generic for
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
was obtained using a well-behaving forcing. In this case, we have better control over the properties of
\mathbb{U}(G)
.

Lemma 41
.

If
\mathbb{R}
is a quite reasonable
(\kappa,\lambda)
-collapse,
H*K
is
\mathbb{R}*\dot{\mathrm{Add}}(\lambda)
-generic, and
G=\psi(H*K)
, where
\psi
is the projection given by Lemma
25
, then in
V[H*K]
,
\mathbb{U}(G)
is
\lambda
-strategically closed.

Proof.

Let
\langle\mathbb{R}_{\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda\rangle
and
\langle\rho_{\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda\rangle
witness that
\mathbb{R}
is quite reasonable. Suppose Player I plays
u_{0}=\langle\langle\langle\mathbb{Q}_{i},1,\tau_{i}\rangle:i\leq\xi_{0}\rangle,\langle\pi_{ji}:i<j\leq\xi_{0}\rangle,\langle q_{ji}:i<j\leq\xi_{0}\rangle\rangle
. There are
\alpha_{1}<\lambda
,
r_{1}\in\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{1}}
, and
\sigma_{1}
such that
\langle r_{1},\sigma_{1}\rangle\in H*K
and
\psi(r_{1},\sigma_{1})=\langle\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{1}},r_{1},\sigma_{1}\rangle
is a coding condition below
\langle\mathbb{Q}_{\xi_{0}},1,\tau_{\xi_{0}}\rangle
. Using the argument for Lemma
39
,
u_{0}
can be extended to
u_{1}
of length
\xi_{0}+2
with
\langle\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{1}},1,\sigma_{1}\rangle
at position
\xi_{0}+1
. This is Player II’s first move.

Suppose Player I responds with
u_{2}=\langle\langle\langle\mathbb{Q}^{u_{2}}_{i},1,\tau^{u_{2}}_{i}\rangle:i\leq\xi_{2}\rangle,\langle\pi^{u_{2}}_{ji}:i<j\leq\xi_{2}\rangle,\langle q^{u_{2}}_{ji}:i<j\leq\xi_{2}\rangle\rangle
. There are
\alpha_{3}<\lambda
,
r_{3}\in\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{3}}
, and
\sigma_{3}
such that

\langle r_{3},\sigma_{3}\rangle\in H*K
,
r_{3}\Vdash\sigma_{1}\trianglelefteq\sigma_{3}
, and
\psi(r_{3},\sigma_{3})=\langle\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{3}},r_{3},\sigma_{3}\rangle
is a coding condition below
\langle\mathbb{Q}^{u_{2}}_{\xi_{2}},1,\tau^{u_{2}}_{\xi_{2}}\rangle
.
Let
\chi:\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{3}}\to\mathbb{Q}_{\xi_{2}}
witness the ordering.

Let Player II’s next move be
u_{3}=\langle\langle\langle\mathbb{Q}^{u_{3}}_{i},1,\tau^{u_{3}}_{i}\rangle:i\leq\xi_{2}+1\rangle,\langle\pi^{u_{3}}_{ji}:i<j\leq\xi_{2}+1\rangle,\langle q^{u_{3}}_{ji}:i<j\leq\xi_{2}+1\rangle\rangle
, where:

•

\langle\mathbb{Q}^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{2}+1},1,\tau^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{2}+1}\rangle=\langle\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{3}},1,\sigma_{3}\rangle
.

•

\pi^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{2}+1,\beta}=\pi^{u_{2}}_{\xi_{2}\beta}\circ\chi
for
\xi_{0}+1<\beta\leq\xi_{2}
,
\pi^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{2}+1,\xi_{0}+1}=\rho_{\alpha_{1}}\restriction\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{3}}
, and
\pi^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{2}+1,\beta}=\pi^{u_{2}}_{\xi_{0}+1,\beta}\circ\rho_{\alpha_{1}}
for
0\leq\beta\leq\xi_{0}
.

•

For
\xi_{0}+1<\beta\leq\xi_{1}
,
q^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{2}+1,\beta}
is a condition
q
in the generic below
r_{3}
such that
\chi(q)\leq q^{u_{2}}_{\xi_{2},\beta}
, and for
0\leq\beta\leq\xi_{0}+1
,
q^{u_{3}}_{\xi_{2}+1,\beta}
is a condition
q
in the generic below
r_{3}
such that
\rho_{\alpha_{1}}(q)\leq q^{u_{2}}_{\xi_{0}+1,\beta}
.

It is easy to check that condition (
3
) holds for membership in
\mathbb{U}(G)
. The argument that this condition (
4
) is satisfied is the same as for Player II’s second move in Lemma
40
.

Assume inductively that
\delta<\lambda
, and the game has gone on for
\delta
-many rounds, with the following properties:

•

The moves take the form
u_{\beta}=\langle\langle\langle\mathbb{Q}^{u_{\beta}}_{i},1,\tau^{u_{\beta}}_{i}\rangle:i\leq\xi_{\beta}\rangle,\langle\pi^{u_{\beta}}_{ji}:i<j\leq\xi_{\beta}\rangle,\langle q^{u_{\beta}}_{ji}:i<j\leq\xi_{\beta}\rangle\rangle
for

, with Player II having played in odd finite rounds and even infinite rounds.

•

There is an increasing sequence of ordinals
\langle\alpha_{\beta}:\beta<\delta\rangle
such that for odd finite and even infinite
\beta
,
\mathbb{Q}^{u_{\beta}}_{\xi_{\beta}}=\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{\beta}}
.

•

For
\beta

, and
\tau_{\xi_{\beta}}
is an
\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{\beta}}
-name for a binary sequence of length

(so that

).

•

For
\beta<\gamma
both rounds where II played,
\pi^{u_{\beta}}_{\xi_{\gamma}\xi_{\beta}}=\rho_{\alpha_{\beta}}\restriction\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{\gamma}}
.

If
\delta
is not a limit and the last round was a move of Player I, then II responds just as in round 3.

Let
\delta
be a limit ordinal. The cofinality of
\delta
in

is at most
\kappa
. Let

, and let

for an

is a coding condition.
Put

.
Let us define the condition

extending the amalgamation of

for

. So

and

•

\mathbb{Q}^{u_{\delta}}_{\xi_{\delta}}=\mathbb{R}_{\alpha_{\delta}}
and

.

•

For
\beta<\delta
odd finite or even infinite, put

•

For
\beta<\xi_{\delta}
not of the form
\xi_{\beta}
for
\beta

be least such that

, and let

. Since

.

•

Suppose
\beta<\xi_{\delta}
is not an index of a Player II round. Let
i

\gamma_{i}>\beta
.
Let
q^{u_{\delta}}_{\xi_{\delta},\beta}
be a condition in the generic such that its projection under
\pi^{u_{\delta}}_{\xi_{\delta},\eta}
is stronger than
q^{u_{\gamma_{i}}}_{\eta,\zeta}
for all

.

Let us verify that under this construction,
u_{\delta}
is a condition in
\mathbb{U}(G)
.
To show (
4
), suppose
x\in[\xi_{\delta}]^{<\kappa}
, and let
x^{\prime}=x\cup\{\xi_{\delta}\}
.
Assume
q\in E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction x^{\prime},\vec{\pi}\restriction x^{\prime}\rangle}_{\xi_{\delta}}
,

\pi_{\xi_{\delta},\beta}(q)\leq q_{\beta\alpha}
for all
\{\alpha,\beta\}\in[x^{\prime}]^{2}
.
Let
y=\{i<\nu:(\exists\beta\in x)i
is least such that
\xi_{\gamma_{i}}\geq\beta\}
.
By construction, we also have
q\in E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction(x^{\prime}\cup y),\vec{\pi}\restriction(x^{\prime}\cup y)\rangle}_{\xi_{\delta}}
, and

\pi_{\xi_{\delta},\beta}(q)\leq q_{\beta\alpha}
for all

Suppose
\vec{r}\leq\langle\pi_{\xi_{\delta},\beta}(q):\beta\in x\rangle
in
\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction x,\vec{\pi}\restriction x)
.
If
y
has a maximum element

is a condition, there is

such that

. Then we take

in

such that
\pi_{\xi_{\delta},\xi_{\gamma_{j}}}(q^{\prime\prime})\leq q^{\prime}
.

If

. By Lemma

, there is

is canonically isomorphic to

, and then find

in

such that

. Thus
\langle\pi_{\xi_{\delta},\beta}(q^{\prime\prime}):\beta\in x\rangle\leq\vec{r}
.

Corollary 42
.

\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
forces that
\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
is
\lambda
-distributive.

Proof.

Suppose
G\subseteq\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
is generic, and towards a contradiction, suppose
\alpha<\lambda
and
u\in\mathbb{U}(G)
forces
\dot{x}
to be a new
\alpha
-sequence of ordinals over
V[G]
. Force a generic
H*K\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)*\dot{\mathrm{Add}}(\lambda)
such that
\psi(H{*}K)=G
. By Lemma
28
,
V[H{*}K]
has the same
{<}\lambda
-sequences of ordinals as
V[G]
. In
V[H{*}K]
,
\mathbb{U}(G)
is
\lambda
-distributive, so if
U\subseteq\mathbb{U}(G)
is a generic filter over
V[H{*}K]
with
u\in U
, then
V[H{*}K][U]
has the same
{<}\lambda
-sequences of ordinals as
V[G]
. Thus
\dot{x}^{U}\in V[G]
, but since
U
is also generic over
V[G]
and
u\Vdash\dot{x}\notin V[G]
, this is a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 43

(Master conditions)
.

\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\lambda)
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,\lambda)
. Moreover, whenever
\lambda^{\prime}>\lambda
is inaccessible, there are
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,\lambda)
-names
\tau^{*}
and
\dot{u}^{*}
such that
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,\lambda),1,\tau^{*}\rangle
is a strong coding condition in
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda^{\prime})
and
\dot{u}^{*}
is forced to be an element of
\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G}^{\prime})
, where
\dot{G}^{\prime}
is the canonical name for the
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda^{\prime})
-generic, with the following property:

Whenever
G^{\prime}*U^{\prime}\subseteq\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda^{\prime})*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G}^{\prime})
is a generic with
\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,\lambda),1,\tau^{*}\rangle,\dot{u}^{*}\rangle\in G^{\prime}*U^{\prime}
, and
G*U*K
is the
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\lambda)
-generic induced by
(G^{\prime})^{\tau^{*}}_{\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,\lambda)}
, then
G*U\subseteq G^{\prime}*U^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We combine the ideas of Lemmas
11
and
40
. The forcing
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\lambda)
collapses
\lambda
to
\kappa
, and it has a dense subset of size
\lambda
.
11
11

11

Because of the lack of
\lambda
-c.c., the iteration does not have size
\lambda
, but the claim holds since each iterand is forced to have size
\lambda
.
Let
\dot{f}
be a name for a surjection from
\kappa
to the generic filter intersected with this dense set.

We use the strategy
\Sigma
for Player II from Lemma
40
to build a dense tree
T
in the Boolean completion of the three-step iteration that is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,\lambda)
. For the first level of the tree, note that there is a dense set of conditions
\langle s,u,c\rangle\in\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\lambda)
such that
\langle s,u\rangle=\Sigma(\langle\langle s^{\prime},u^{\prime}\rangle\rangle)
for some
\langle s^{\prime},u^{\prime}\rangle
, and
\langle s,u,c\rangle\leq q
for some
q
such that
\langle s,u,c\rangle\Vdash\dot{f}(0)=\check{q}
. Take the first level
T_{1}
to be a maximal antichain of size
\lambda
of such conditions.

Assume inductively that we have constructed the levels
\langle T_{\beta}:\beta<\alpha\rangle
with the following properties:

•

For
\gamma<\beta<\alpha
,
T_{\beta}
is a maximal antichain refining
T_{\gamma}
.

•

If
\beta<\alpha
is a successor, then the elements of
T_{\beta}
are members of
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\lambda)
, and if
\beta<\alpha
is a limit, then each
p\in T_{\beta}
is of the form
\inf\{q\in\bigcup_{\gamma<\beta}T_{\gamma}:q\geq p\}
.

•

For each successor
\beta<\alpha
and each
p\in T_{\beta}
, we have assigned a descending sequence
\langle\langle s_{i}^{p},u_{i}^{p},c_{i}^{p}\rangle:i<2\beta\rangle\subseteq\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\lambda)
with minimum element
p=\langle s_{2\beta-1}^{p},u_{2\beta-1}^{p},c_{2\beta-1}^{p}\rangle
, such that
\langle\langle s_{i}^{p},u_{i}^{p}\rangle:i<2\beta\rangle
is a partial play of
\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{I}}(\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G}))
following
\Sigma
, with the property that
\{\langle s_{i}^{p},u_{i}^{p},c_{i}^{p}\rangle:i<2\beta
is odd
\}
is exactly the set of nodes
q\in T_{\gamma}
for successor
\gamma<\beta
that are above
p
. Furthermore, we require that for successor
\gamma<\beta<\alpha
,
q\in T_{\gamma}
, and
p\in T_{\beta}
such that
q\geq p
,
\langle\langle s_{i}^{p},u_{i}^{p},c_{i}^{p}\rangle:i<2\gamma\rangle
is an initial segment of
\langle\langle s_{i}^{p},u_{i}^{p},c_{i}^{p}\rangle:i<2\beta\rangle
.

•

If
\beta<\alpha
and
\beta=\gamma+1
, then for each
p\in T_{\beta}
, there is some
q
such that
p\leq q
and
p\Vdash\dot{f}(\gamma)=\check{q}
.

If
\alpha
is a limit, let
T_{\alpha}
be the set of infima to the branches through
\bigcup_{\beta<\alpha}T_{\beta}
. Since the first two coordinates along each such branch conform to a play of the game according to
\Sigma
, and the third coordinate is in a
\kappa
-closed forcing, these infima are nonzero.
T_{\alpha}
is a maximal antichain just as in Lemma
11
.

If
\alpha=\beta+1
, then for each
p\in T_{\beta}
, we can find
\langle s,u,c\rangle=p^{\prime}\leq p
such that for some
q
,
p^{\prime}\leq q
and
p^{\prime}\Vdash\dot{f}(\beta)=\check{q}
. We can put
\langle s,u\rangle
as Player I’s next move in the game. Thus there is a dense set of
r\leq p
whose first two coordinates come as Player II’s next move in the game according to
\Sigma
with initial sequence of plays
\langle\langle s_{i}^{p},u_{i}^{p},c_{i}^{p}\rangle:i<2\beta\rangle
. Let
T^{p}_{\alpha}
be a maximal antichain of such conditions, and for each
r\in T^{p}_{\alpha}
, choose
\langle s^{r}_{2\beta},u^{r}_{2\beta},c^{r}_{2\beta}\rangle
to give moves for Player I at round
2\beta
, continuing the sequence of plays up to
r
. Let
T_{\alpha}=\bigcup\{T^{p}_{\alpha}:p\in T_{\beta}\}
. This completes the induction.

T
is dense because for any
p\in\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\lambda)
, there are conditions
q
and
r
and some
\alpha<\kappa
such that
r\leq p
and
q\Vdash\dot{f}(\alpha)=\check{r}
. There is some
t\in T_{\alpha+1}
compatible with
q
, and by construction we must have
t\leq r\leq p
.

Let us now define a system of projections from dense open subsets of
T
to
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha)
for
\alpha<\lambda
. Recall that all of Player II’s moves according to
\Sigma
take the form
\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha),p,\tau\rangle,\dot{u}\rangle
. For
\alpha<\lambda
, let
D_{\alpha}
be the set of nodes
t\in T
such that either
t
is a successor node with the first coordinate of the form
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta),p,\tau\rangle
for some
\beta\geq\alpha
, or
t
is a limit node, and
\alpha\leq\sup z
, where
z
is the set of ordinals
\beta
such that there is a successor node above
t
with first coordinate of the form
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta),p,\tau\rangle
.
If
t\in D_{\alpha}
is a successor node of the form
\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta),p,\tau\rangle,u,c\rangle
, let
\sigma_{\alpha}(t)=p\restriction\alpha
.
To show this is order-preserving, suppose
t_{0}>t_{1}
are successor nodes of
T
, with the first coordinate of
t_{i}
being
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta_{i}),q_{i},\tau_{i}\rangle
. Since these nodes conform to a play of the game by
\Sigma
, the restriction map
x\mapsto x\restriction\beta_{0}
witnesses the ordering
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta_{1}),q_{1},\tau_{1}\rangle\leq\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta_{0}),q_{0},\tau_{0}\rangle
. Thus
q_{1}\supseteq q_{0}
, and so
q_{1}\restriction\alpha\supseteq q_{0}\restriction\alpha
.

If
t\in D_{\alpha}
is a limit node, let
\delta
be the ordertype of the set of nodes above
t
, and let
\langle\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta_{i}),q_{i},\tau_{i}\rangle,u_{i},c_{i}\rangle:i<\delta\rangle
list the successor nodes above
t
. Put
\sigma_{\alpha}(t)=\bigcup_{i}q_{i}\restriction\alpha
.
By definition,
\sigma_{\alpha}
is
\kappa
-continuous, and order preservation also holds for pairs that may include limit nodes. If
\beta<\alpha
and
t\in D_{\alpha}
, then
t\in D_{\beta}
also, and
\sigma_{\beta}(t)=\sigma_{\alpha}(t)\restriction\beta
.

To show
\sigma_{\alpha}
is a projection, suppose
\alpha<\lambda
,
t\in D_{\alpha}
, and
p\leq\sigma_{\alpha}(t)
. If
t
is a successor node, then
t
has the form
\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta),q,\tau\rangle,u,c\rangle
. We get a stronger condition by replacing
q
with
p\cup q
, and since
T
is dense, there is a successor node in
T
below it of the form
t^{\prime}=\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\gamma),r,\tau^{\prime}\rangle,u^{\prime},c^{\prime}\rangle
, for some
\gamma\geq\beta
. Let
\pi
witness the ordering
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\gamma),r,\tau^{\prime}\rangle\leq\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta),p\cup q,\tau\rangle
. Since the branch up to
t^{\prime}
conforms to
\Sigma
,
the restriction map
x\mapsto x\restriction\beta
witnesses the ordering
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\gamma),r,\tau^{\prime}\rangle\leq\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta),q,\tau\rangle
. Thus both
(\cdot\restriction\beta)
and
\mathrm{id}\circ\pi=\pi
witness this ordering, so by Lemma
31
,
\pi(r)=r\restriction\beta
.
Thus
r\supseteq p\cup q
, and so
\sigma_{\alpha}(t^{\prime})\leq p
.

If
t
is a limit node, let
\langle\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta_{i}),q_{i},\tau_{i}\rangle,u_{i},c_{i}\rangle:i<\delta\rangle
list the successor nodes above
t
. Let
\gamma=\sup_{i}\beta_{i}
. As in the limit case of the proof of Lemma
40
, one lower bound to the branch above
t
takes the form
\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\gamma),q,\tau\rangle,u,c\rangle
, where
q=\bigcup_{i}q_{i}
. Thus
\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\gamma),p\cup q,\tau\rangle,u,c\rangle
is another lower bound, which is a condition stronger than
t
. Since
T
is dense, there is a successor node in
T
below it of the form
t^{\prime}=\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\eta),r,\tau^{\prime}\rangle,u^{\prime},c^{\prime}\rangle
.
Let
\pi
be a projection witnessing
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\eta,r,\tau^{\prime}\rangle\leq\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\gamma),p\cup q,\tau\rangle
.
Since the branch up to
t^{\prime}
conforms to
\Sigma
, the restriction map witnesses the ordering
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\eta),r,\tau^{\prime}\rangle\leq\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta_{i}),q_{i},\tau_{i}\rangle
for
i<\delta
.
For such
i
, the projections
(\cdot\restriction\beta_{i})\circ\pi
and
(\cdot\restriction\beta_{i})
both witness
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\eta),r,\tau^{\prime}\rangle\leq\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta_{i}),q_{i},\tau_{i}\rangle
, so by Lemma
31
, they are the same below
r
. Thus
\pi(r)\restriction\gamma=r\restriction\gamma
, so
r\supseteq p\cup q
, and
\sigma_{\alpha}(t^{\prime})\leq p
.

Let
\tau_{0}
be a
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
-name for the generic function
g:\lambda\to 2
as in the proof of Lemma
28
. Then let
\tau^{*}
be a
T
-name for a binary function of length
\lambda+\lambda
, where
\tau^{*}\restriction\lambda=\tau_{0}
, and
\tau^{*}\restriction[\lambda,\lambda+\lambda)
codes the generic for
T
(as in the definition of strong coding). It is clear that for any inaccessible
\lambda^{\prime}>\lambda
,
\langle T,1,\tau^{*}\rangle
is a coding condition in
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda^{\prime})
. We claim that for any
t=\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha),p,\tau\rangle,u,c\rangle\in T
, the projection
\sigma_{\alpha}
witnesses that
\langle T,t,\tau^{*}\rangle\leq\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha),p,\tau\rangle
.
Suppose
B\subseteq T
is generic with
t\in B
,
H=\sigma_{\alpha}(B)
, and
G*U*K\subseteq\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(G^{\prime})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\lambda)
is the filter corresponding to
B
.
Since
\sigma_{\alpha}(t)=p
,
p\in H
.
Further, for all
q\in H
, there is
s=\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta),p^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}\rangle,u^{\prime},c^{\prime}\rangle\in B
below
t
such that
\sigma_{\alpha}(s)=p^{\prime}\restriction\alpha\leq q
. Then
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta),p^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}\rangle\in G
, and
p^{\prime}\restriction\alpha\in G^{\tau}_{\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,<\alpha)}
. Thus
H\subseteq G^{\tau}_{\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,<\alpha)}
, and by the maximality of generic filters,
H=G^{\tau}_{\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,<\alpha)}
. If
\delta
is the forced domain of
\tau
, then
\tau^{H}=\tau_{0}^{G}\restriction\delta=(\tau^{*})^{B}\restriction\delta
.

Suppose
G^{\prime}\subseteq\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda^{\prime})
is generic with
\langle T,1,\tau^{*}\rangle\in G^{\prime}
,
B
is the induced generic branch for
T
, and
G*U*K\subseteq\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(G^{\prime})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\lambda)
is the corresponding filter. For any
\langle\mathbb{P},p,\tau\rangle\in G
, there is a successor node
t=\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha),p^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}\rangle,u,c\rangle\in B
such that
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha),p^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}\rangle\leq\langle\mathbb{P},p,\tau\rangle
. Since
\langle T,t,\tau^{*}\rangle\leq\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha),p^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}\rangle
, we have
\langle\mathbb{P},p,\tau\rangle\in G^{\prime}
as well. Hence,
G\subseteq G^{\prime}
.

Let
\dot{u}^{*}=\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}^{u^{*}},\vec{\pi}^{u^{*}},\vec{q}^{u^{*}}\rangle
be a
T
-name for

\langle\langle\langle\mathbb{Q}_{i},1,\tau_{i}\rangle:i\leq\lambda\rangle,\langle\pi_{ji}:i<j\leq\lambda\rangle,\langle q_{ji}:i<j\leq\lambda\rangle,

defined as follows. Let
\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction\lambda
,
\vec{\pi}\restriction\lambda
and
\vec{q}\restriction\lambda
be the unions of the corresponding coordinates of conditions appearing in the
\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
-generic. Let
\mathbb{Q}_{\lambda}=T
and
\tau_{\lambda}=\tau^{*}
. Let
\langle\alpha_{i}:i<\kappa\rangle
enumerate the indices in
\vec{\mathbb{Q}}
at which the posets in the first coordinates of the successor nodes of
T
appear in the generic branch. For

i<\kappa
, if
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha_{i}}=\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta)
, let
\pi_{\lambda\alpha_{i}}=\sigma_{\beta}
.
For
\gamma<\lambda
such that
\gamma\notin\{\alpha_{i}:i<\kappa\}
, let
i<\kappa
be least such that
\gamma<\alpha_{i}
, and let
\pi_{\lambda\gamma}=\pi_{\alpha_{i}\gamma}\circ\pi_{\lambda\alpha_{i}}
, restricted to the set of points
t
such that
\pi_{\gamma\alpha_{j}}\circ\pi_{\alpha_{i}\gamma}\circ\pi_{\lambda\alpha_{i}}(t)
is defined for all
j<i
.

To define the conditions
q_{\lambda\gamma}
for
\gamma<\lambda
, first for
i<\kappa
, let
q_{\lambda\alpha_{i}}
be the
i^{th}
successor node
t\in B
, so
t
has the form
\langle\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta),p,\tau\rangle,u,c\rangle
. We established above that
\sigma_{\beta}
witnesses
\langle T,t,\tau^{*}\rangle\leq\langle\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta),p,\tau\rangle
.
For
\gamma<\lambda
such that
\gamma\notin\{\alpha_{i}:i<\kappa\}
, let
i
be least such that
\alpha_{i}>\gamma
. Let
q_{\lambda\gamma}
be some
t\in B
such that
t\leq q_{\lambda\alpha_{i}}
, and
\pi_{\lambda\beta}(t)\leq q_{\beta\alpha}
for all
\{\alpha,\beta\}\subseteq\{\gamma\}\cup\{\alpha_{j}:j\leq i\}
.

As
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda^{\prime})\restriction\langle T,1,\tau^{*}\rangle
induces a generic for
T
,
\dot{u}^{*}
can be interpreted as a
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda^{\prime})
-name. We must check that
\dot{u}^{*}
is forced to be a member of
\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
. The satisfaction of the first three clauses of the definition is clear.
For (
4
), suppose
B
and
G*U*K
are generics as above, and let
u^{*}=(\dot{u}^{*})^{B}
. Suppose
x\in[\lambda]^{<\kappa}
, and let
x^{\prime}=x\cup\{\lambda\}
. Let
\langle\alpha_{i}:i<\kappa\rangle
be as above, and let
y=\{\alpha_{i}:(\exists\beta\in x)\alpha_{i}
is least such that
\alpha_{i}\geq\beta\}
. Suppose
t\in E_{\lambda}^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction x^{\prime},\vec{\pi}\restriction x^{\prime}\rangle}
is such that
\pi_{\lambda\beta}(t)\leq q_{\beta\alpha}
for all
\{\alpha,\beta\}\in[x^{\prime}]^{2}
. By construction, we also have that
\pi_{\lambda\beta}(t)\leq q_{\beta\alpha}
for all
\{\alpha,\beta\}\in[x^{\prime}\cup y]^{2}
.
Assume
\vec{r}\in\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction x,\vec{\pi}\restriction x)
and
\vec{r}\leq\langle\pi_{\lambda i}(t):i\in x\rangle
.

Suppose first that
y
has a maximum element
\alpha_{j}
. Then there is
u\in U
such that
u^{*}\restriction(\alpha_{j}+1)=u
, and we have that, putting
q=\pi_{\lambda\alpha_{j}}(t)
,
\pi_{\alpha_{j}\gamma}(q)\leq q_{\gamma\beta}
for all
\{\beta,\gamma\}\in[x\cup y]^{2}
. Thus there is
q^{\prime}\leq q
such that

\pi_{\alpha_{j}\gamma}(q^{\prime})\leq\vec{r}
, and there is
t^{\prime}\leq t
in
E_{\lambda}^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction x^{\prime},\vec{\pi}\restriction x^{\prime}\rangle}
such that
\pi_{\lambda\alpha_{j}}(t^{\prime})\leq q^{\prime}
.

Suppose next that
y
does not have a maximal element. By Lemma
18
, there is
\vec{s}\in\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction(x\cup y),\vec{\pi}\restriction(x\cup y))
such that
\vec{s}\restriction x\leq\vec{r}
.
Let
z
be the set of ordinals
\beta
such that
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\beta)
appears as
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha_{i}}
for
\alpha_{i}\in y
, and let
\gamma=\sup z
.
Then
\vec{s}\restriction y
can be represented as
p\in\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\gamma)
via the canonical isomorphism.
We have that
t\in\dom\sigma_{\gamma}
, and
p\leq\sigma_{\gamma}(t)
. By the fact that
\sigma_{\gamma}
is a projection, there is
t^{\prime}\leq t
in
E_{\lambda}^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction x^{\prime},\vec{\pi}\restriction x^{\prime}\rangle}
such that
\sigma_{\gamma}(t^{\prime})\leq p
, which implies
\langle\pi_{\lambda i}(t^{\prime}):i\in x\rangle\leq\vec{r}
.

Finally, if we force with
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda^{\prime})*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G}^{\prime})
below
\langle\langle T,1,\tau^{*}\rangle,\dot{u}^{*}\rangle
, obtaining a generic
G^{\prime}*U^{\prime}
, then if
G*U
is the generic for
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
induced from the
T
-generic, then all elements of
U
are initial segments of
(\dot{u}^{*})^{G^{\prime}}\restriction\lambda
, so
U\subseteq U^{\prime}
.
∎

5
Dense ideals on
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{2}

Let us first show how the posets thus far introduced can force the existence of dense ideals on successors of regular uncountable cardinals. Recall that a cardinal
\kappa
is
almost-huge
if there is an elementary embedding
j:V\to M
with critical point
\kappa
such that
M^{<j(\kappa)}\subseteq M
. We say, “
\kappa
is almost-huge with target
\lambda
,” when there is an embedding
j
witnessing the almost-hugeness of
\kappa
with
j(\kappa)=\lambda
. If
j:V\to M
is such an embedding, then by
[
19
, Theorem 24.11]
, there is another embedding
i:V\to N
witnessing the almost-hugeness of
\kappa
, with
i(\kappa)=\lambda
, given via the direct limit of supercompactness measures derived from
j
. Such an embedding
i
has the additional property that
\lambda<i(\lambda)<\lambda^{+}
and
i[\lambda]
is cofinal in
i(\lambda)
.

Theorem 44
.

Suppose
\kappa
is almost-huge with target
\lambda
and
\mu<\kappa
is regular and uncountable. Then
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)
forces that there is a normal ideal
I
on
\kappa
such that
\mathcal{P}(\kappa)/I\cong\mathcal{B}(\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa))
.

Proof.

Let
j:V\to M
be an embedding witnessing that
\kappa
is almost-huge with target
\lambda
. Let us assume that
j(\lambda)<\lambda^{+}
and
j[\lambda]
is cofinal in
j(\lambda)
.

By Lemma
43
,
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\mu,\kappa)
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
, so
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)
can be seen as a subforcing of
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
. The iteration
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)^{\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)}
is clearly a quite reasonable
(\mu,\lambda)
-collapse. Let us choose an increasing sequence of regular suborders witnessing this,
\langle\mathbb{R}_{\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda\rangle
, with the property that
\mathbb{R}_{0}=\{1\}
and
\mathbb{R}_{\alpha}=\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
for
0<\alpha\leq\kappa+\kappa
, for the map
\psi
from Lemma
25
to satisfy that
\psi(1,\tau^{*})=\langle\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa),1,\tau^{*}\rangle
, where
\tau^{*}
is the name for the
(\kappa+\kappa)
-length binary sequence given by Lemma
43
. The later
\mathbb{R}_{\alpha}
are of the form
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,{<}\beta_{\alpha})^{\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)}
for some appropriate increasing sequence of ordinals
\beta_{\alpha}
, and the witnessing system of projection maps is given by the canonical restriction maps.

Let
G*U*H\subseteq\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)
be generic. Let
g\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
be generic over
V[G*U*H]
, which implies that
H
is generic over
V[G*U*g]
. By Lemma
43
,
G*U*g
is equivalent to a generic
K\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
so
G*U*g*H
induces a
V
-generic
K*H\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)^{\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)}
.

Now consider the model
M[K{*}H]
. Since in
M
,
j(\lambda)
is an inaccessible cardinal below
(\lambda^{+})^{V}
,
\mathcal{P}(\lambda)^{M[K*H]}
has size
\lambda
in
V[K{*}H]
. Also, since
K*H
is generic for a forcing that is
\lambda
-c.c. in
M
,
M[K{*}H]
is
{<}\lambda
-closed in
V[K{*}H]
. Working in
V[K{*}H]
, inductively build a filter
h\subseteq\mathrm{Add}(\lambda)^{M[K*H]}
that is generic over
M[K{*}H]
with
(\tau^{*})^{K}\in h
.

Let
\psi:[\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)^{\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)}]*\dot{\mathrm{Add}}(\lambda)\to\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\lambda)
be the projection of Lemma
25
, and let
G^{\prime}=\psi(K{*}H{*}h)
. We have
\langle\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa),1,\tau^{*}\rangle\in G^{\prime}
,
K=(G^{\prime})^{\tau^{*}}_{\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)}
, and by Lemma
43
,
G\subseteq G^{\prime}
. Therefore, in
V[K{*}H]
, we can lift the embedding to
j:V[G]\to M[G^{\prime}]
. By Lemma
28
,
M[G^{\prime}]
is
{<}\lambda
-closed in
M[K{*}H]
, so it is
{<}\lambda
-closed in
V[K{*}H]
.

Let
u^{*}
be the condition in
\mathbb{U}(G^{\prime})
given by Lemma
43
. Using the closure of
M[G^{\prime}]
, the fact that
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{U}(G^{\prime}))^{M[G^{\prime}]}
has size
\lambda
in
V[K{*}H]
, and the
\lambda
-strategic closure given by Lemma
41
, build a filter
U^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{U}(G^{\prime})
that is generic over
M[G^{\prime}]
with
u^{*}\in U^{\prime}
. By Lemma
43
,
U\subseteq U^{\prime}
. Thus the embedding can be further lifted to
j:V[G{*}U]\to M[G^{\prime}{*}U^{\prime}]
.

For each
\alpha<\lambda
, let
H_{\alpha}=H\cap\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\alpha)^{V[G*U]}
. Each such
H_{\alpha}
is a member of
M[G^{\prime}]
. Let
m_{\alpha}=\bigcup j[H_{\alpha}]\in M[G^{\prime}]
, which is a condition in
\mathrm{Col}(\lambda,{<}j(\lambda))^{M[G^{\prime}]}
since
|H_{\alpha}|<\lambda
and the Levy collapse is
\lambda
-directed closed. In
V[K{*}H]
, let
\langle A_{\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda\rangle
be an enumeration of the maximal antichains contained in
\mathrm{Col}(\lambda,{<}j(\lambda))^{M[G^{\prime}]}
that are members of
M[G^{\prime}{*}U^{\prime}]
, which is possible again because
j(\lambda)<(\lambda^{+})^{V}
.

Recursively, build a descending sequence
\langle q_{\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda\rangle
such that
q_{\alpha}\leq m_{\alpha}
and
q_{\alpha}\in D_{\alpha}
. In order to do this, also recursively choose an increasing sequence of ordinals
\langle\gamma_{\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda\rangle\subseteq\lambda
such that
\gamma_{\alpha}\geq\alpha
and
A_{\alpha}\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}j(\gamma_{\alpha}))^{M[G^{\prime}]}
. We inductively assume that
q_{\alpha}
is compatible with
j(p)
for all
p\in H
and that
q_{\alpha}\in\mathrm{Col}(\lambda,{<}j(\gamma_{\alpha}))
. Given
q_{\alpha}
, first take
q_{\alpha+1}^{\prime}=q_{\alpha}\cup m_{\gamma_{\alpha+1}}
, and then find
q_{\alpha+1}\leq q_{\alpha+1}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Col}(\lambda,{<}j(\gamma_{\alpha+1}))^{M[G^{\prime}]}
such that
q_{\alpha+1}\leq a
for some
a\in A_{\alpha}
.
q_{\alpha+1}
is compatible with
j(p)
for all
p\in H
because
q_{\alpha+1}\supseteq j(p)
for all
p\in H_{\gamma_{\alpha+1}}
.
At a limit stage
\alpha
, given
\langle q_{i}:i<\alpha\rangle
, let
q_{\alpha}=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}q_{i}
, which is compatible with
j(p)
for all
p\in H
because each
q_{i}
is.

The sequence
\langle q_{\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda\rangle
generates a filter
H^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\lambda,j(\lambda))^{M[G^{\prime}]}
that is generic over
M[G^{\prime}]
and has the property that
j[H]\subseteq H^{\prime}
.

Thus, we may lift the embedding one more time to
j:V[G{*}U{*}H]\to M[G^{\prime}{*}U^{\prime}{*}H^{\prime}]
. To perform this lifting, we only needed to adjoin the generic
g\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
to
V[G{*}U{*}H]
. In
V[G{*}U{*}H]
, define

I=\{X\subseteq\kappa:1\Vdash_{\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)}\check{\kappa}\notin j(X)\}.

By standard arguments,
I
is
\kappa
-complete and normal.

Claim 45
.

There is a dense embedding
e
from
\mathcal{P}(\kappa)/I
to
\mathcal{B}(\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa))
.

Proof.

Define
e:\mathcal{P}(\kappa)/I\to\mathcal{B}(\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa))
by
e([X]_{I})=\|\check{\kappa}\in j(X)\|
. It is clear that
e
preserves order and antichains.

Since
|\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)|=\kappa
,
\mathcal{P}(X)/I
is
\lambda
-c.c. If
\langle[X_{\alpha}]_{I}:\alpha<\kappa\rangle
is a maximal antichain, then by normality,
\nabla_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}:=\{\beta:(\exists\alpha<\beta)\beta\in X_{\alpha}\}
is equivalent to
\kappa
modulo
I
, and thus
1_{\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)}
forces that
\kappa\in j(X_{\alpha})
for some
\alpha<\kappa
; in other words
\{e([X_{\alpha}]_{I}):\alpha<\kappa\}
is maximal in
\mathcal{B}(\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa))
. Thus
e
is a complete embedding.

In order to show that
e
is dense, it suffices to show that there is a name
\sigma
in the forcing language of
\mathcal{P}(\kappa)/I
such that whenever
g\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
is generic and
F=e^{-1}[g]
, then
g=\sigma^{F}
. Indeed, for any
p\in\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
, there is
X\subseteq\kappa
such that
[X]_{I}\Vdash\check{p}\in\sigma
, and so
e([X]_{I})\leq p
.

To show the desired claim, let
g\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
be generic, let
F=e^{-1}[g]
, and let
G^{\prime},U^{\prime},H^{\prime}
be as above. Let
N=\Ult(V[G{*}U{*}H],F)
, let
i:V[G{*}U{*}H]\to N
be the ultrapower embedding, and let
k:N\to M[G^{\prime}{*}U^{\prime}{*}H^{\prime}]
be defined by
k([h]_{F})=j(h)(\kappa)
. Then
k
is elementary and
j=k\circ i
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V[G{*}U{*}H]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M[G^{\prime}{*}U^{\prime}{*}H^{\prime}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}

Since
I
is normal and
\kappa
-complete,
\crit(i)=\kappa
. In particular, for every
X\subseteq\kappa
in
V[G{*}U{*}H]
,
X\in N
and thus
(\kappa^{+})^{N}\geq\lambda
. Since
\crit(k)>\kappa
(as
\kappa=k([\mathrm{id}]_{F})
), and
\crit(k)
must be a cardinal in
N
,
\crit k\geq\lambda
. Since
k(i(\kappa))=j(\kappa)=\lambda
, in fact
\crit(k)>\lambda
. This implies that
i(G)=j(G)=G^{\prime}
.

Thus,
g
can be defined as the filter over the third coordinate given by the translating
i(G)^{\tau^{*}}_{\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)}
into a filter over
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\mu,\kappa)
via the embedding of Lemma
43
.
∎

So,
e^{-1}(\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa))
is dense in the
I
-positive subsets of
\kappa
, as wanted.
∎

The basic arguments about
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)
, even the transitivity of the order, only work when
\mu
is regular and uncountable. Thus, a different approach is needed for dense ideals on
\omega_{1}
. Fortunately, suitable technology has already been presented in
[
7
]
.

For regular cardinals
\mu<\kappa
, the
anonymous collapse

\mathbb{A}(\mu,\kappa)
is defined as follows. Consider the complete Boolean algebra
\mathbb{B}=\mathcal{B}(\mathrm{Col}(\mu,{<}\kappa)*\dot{\mathrm{Add}}(\kappa))
. Let
\dot{X}
be the canonical name for the generic subset of
\kappa
added by the second step. Then let
\mathbb{A}(\mu,\kappa)
be the complete subalgebra generated by
\{\|\check{\alpha}\in\dot{X}\|:\alpha<\kappa\}
. When
\mu
is uncountable,
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)
corresponds to a complete subalgebra
\mathbb{S}\subseteq\mathbb{B}
, and by the argument for Lemma
28
,
\mathbb{A}(\mu,\kappa)\subseteq\mathbb{S}
. But, unlike our version of the Shioya forcing,
\mathbb{A}(\mu,\kappa)
makes sense also for
\mu=\omega
.

The following lemma has been known in the folklore for a long time—see for example
[
10
, p. 27]
.
A proof for a more general class of forcing notions is given in
[
7
]
.

Lemma 46
.

If
\mathbb{P}
is a reasonable
(\mu,\kappa)
-collapse, then whenever
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}
is generic, there is some further forcing extension
W\supseteq V[G]
in which there exists a
V
-generic
H\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,{<}\kappa)
such that
\mathcal{P}(\mu)^{V[H]}=\mathcal{P}(\mu)^{V[G]}
.

Theorem 47
.

If
\kappa
is almost-huge with target
\lambda
, then
\mathbb{A}(\omega,\kappa)*\dot{\text{\char 87\relax}}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
forces that there is a normal
\omega_{1}
-dense ideal on
\omega_{1}
.

Proof.

Let
j:V\to M
be an embedding witnessing that
\kappa
is almost-huge with target
\lambda
. Let us assume that
j(\lambda)<\lambda^{+}
and
j[\lambda]
is cofinal in
j(\lambda)
.

Let us fix some generic filters:

•

Let
A*G*U\subseteq\mathbb{A}(\omega,\kappa)*\dot{\text{\char 87\relax}}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
be generic.

•

Let
\mathbb{B}=\mathcal{B}(\mathrm{Col}(\omega,{<}\kappa)*\dot{\mathrm{Add}}(\kappa))/A
, and take a generic
g\times B\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\kappa)\times\mathbb{B}
over
V[A*G*U]
.

•

Let
H*X\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\omega,{<}\kappa)*\dot{\mathrm{Add}}(\kappa)
be the filter corresponding to
A*B
.

Since
B
is generic over
V[A{*}G{*}U{*}g]
,
G*U*g
is generic over
V[A{*}B]=V[H{*}X]
. Since
g\times B
is generic for a forcing of size
\kappa
from
V[A]
, Lemmas
2
and
42
imply that adjoining
U
to
V[A{*}G{*}g{*}B]=V[H{*}X{*}g{*}G]
adds no
{<}\lambda
-sequences. Let
\mathbb{R}_{0}=\mathbb{R}^{V[A]}
, the reals of
V[A]
, and let
\mathbb{R}_{1}=\mathbb{R}^{V[A{*}G{*}g{*}B]}
.

By Lemma
28
, in
V[A]
there is a
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
-name for a
\lambda
-distributive forcing
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{Q}}
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)*\dot{\mathrm{Add}}(\lambda)
.
Again by Lemma
2
,
\mathbb{Q}
remains
\lambda
-distributive in
V[A{*}G{*}g{*}B]
. Thus in some forcing extension of
V[H{*}X{*}g{*}G]
, there is a filter
K\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)^{V[H]}
that is generic over
V[H{*}X{*}g]
and such that
\mathbb{R}^{V[H{*}X{*}g{*}K]}=\mathbb{R}_{1}
. Since
\mathrm{Add}(\kappa)\times\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\kappa)\times\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,{<}\lambda)
is a reasonable
(\omega,\lambda)
-collapse in
V[H]
, Lemma
46
implies that in some further forcing extension, there is a filter
H^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\omega,{<}\lambda)
that is generic over
V[H]
with the property that
\mathbb{R}^{V[H][X*g*K]}=\mathbb{R}^{V[H][H^{\prime\prime}]}
. Rearranging
H\times H^{\prime\prime}
into
H^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\omega,{<}\lambda)
such that
H^{\prime}\restriction\kappa=H
, we have that the embedding lifts to
j:V[H]\to M[H^{\prime}]
.

This embedding restricts to
j:V(\mathbb{R}^{V[H]})\to M(\mathbb{R}^{V[H^{\prime}]})
, which is also elementary, and we have
\mathbb{R}^{V[H]}=\mathbb{R}_{0}
and
\mathbb{R}^{V[H^{\prime}]}=\mathbb{R}_{1}
. Thus the restricted embedding can be defined in
V[A{*}G{*}g{*}B]
.

In
V[A{*}G{*}g{*}B]
,
\mathcal{P}(\lambda)^{M[A{*}G{*}g{*}B]}
has size
\lambda
. Furthermore, for every set of ordinals
x\in V[A{*}G{*}g{*}B]
of size
{<}\lambda
, there is a poset
\mathbb{P}\in V_{\lambda}
, a filter
h\subseteq\mathbb{P}
, and a
\mathbb{P}
-name
\tau\in M
such that
x=\tau^{h}
. Thus
M(\mathbb{R}_{1})
is
{<}\lambda
-closed in
V[A{*}G{*}g{*}B]
.

Using these observations, we can build a set
X^{\prime}\subseteq\lambda
that is
\mathrm{Add}(\lambda)
-generic over
M[A{*}G{*}g{*}B]
and such that
X^{\prime}\cap\kappa=X
. In particular we can further lift the embedding to
j:V(\mathbb{R}_{0})[X]\to M(\mathbb{R}_{1})[X^{\prime}]
.

X
generates the generic
A
, so by elementarity,
X^{\prime}
generates an
M
-generic filter
j(A)=A^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{A}(\omega,\lambda)
.

We will construct a descending sequence
\langle q_{\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda\rangle\subseteq\text{\char 87\relax}(\lambda,j(\lambda))^{M[A^{\prime}]}
such that:

•

for every
p\in G
, there is
\alpha<\lambda
such that
q_{\alpha}\leq j(p)
, and

•

for every
Y\subseteq\kappa
in
V[A{*}G]
and every name
\tau\in V[A]
such that
\tau^{G}=Y
, there is
\alpha<\lambda
such that
q_{\alpha}
decides whether
\kappa\in j(\tau)
.

Then we define an ultrafilter
F
on
\mathcal{P}(\kappa)^{V[A*G]}
by
Y\in F
iff for some name
\tau
such that
\tau^{G}=Y
and some
\alpha<\lambda
,
q_{\alpha}\Vdash\kappa\in j(\tau)
.
F
is well-defined because if
\tau_{0}^{G}=\tau_{1}^{G}
, then there is some
p\in G
such that
p\Vdash\tau_{0}=\tau_{1}
, and then there is some
\alpha<\lambda
such that
q_{\alpha}\Vdash j(\tau_{0})=j(\tau_{1})
.
F
is upwards-closed because if
\tau_{0}^{G}\subseteq\tau_{1}^{G}
, and
\tau_{0}^{G}\in F
, then there is some
p\in G
such that
p\Vdash\tau_{0}\subseteq\tau_{1}
and some large enough
\alpha
such that
q_{\alpha}\Vdash\kappa\in j(\tau_{1})
.
F
is closed under diagonal intersections from
V[A{*}G]
because if
\langle Y_{i}:i<\kappa\rangle\in V[A{*}G]
then there is a sequence of names
\langle\tau_{i}:i<\kappa\rangle\in V[A]
such that
\tau_{i}^{G}=Y_{i}
for
i<\kappa
. If each
Y_{i}\in F
, then there is some
\alpha<\lambda
such that
q_{\alpha}\Vdash\kappa\in j(\tau_{i})
for
i<\kappa
, and it follows that
q_{\alpha}\Vdash\kappa\in j(\triangle_{i}\tau_{i})
.

F
is “ultra” because for every relevant name
\tau
, there is some
\alpha<\lambda
such that either
q_{\alpha}\Vdash\kappa\in j(\tau)
or
q_{\alpha}\Vdash\kappa\in\lambda\setminus j(\tau)
.

Let
\langle\langle\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},1,\tau_{\alpha}\rangle:\alpha<\lambda\rangle,\langle\pi_{\beta\alpha}:\alpha<\beta<\lambda\rangle,\langle q_{\beta\alpha}:\alpha<\beta<\lambda\rangle\rangle
be the uniformizing sequence obtained from the generic filter
U
.
For each
\alpha<\lambda
, let
m_{\alpha}\in j(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha})
be
\inf\{j(p):p\in G^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}}\}
. For
\alpha<\beta
, the genericity of
G^{\tau_{\beta}}_{\mathbb{P}_{\beta}}
and the density of
\dom\pi_{\beta\alpha}
imply that
\dom\pi_{\beta\alpha}\cap G^{\tau_{\beta}}_{\mathbb{P}_{\beta}}
is a downwards-dominating subset of
G^{\tau_{\beta}}_{\mathbb{P}_{\beta}}
. It follows by the
\kappa
-closedness of
\dom\pi_{\beta\alpha}
that
m_{\beta}\in\dom j(\pi_{\beta\alpha})
, and by continuity that
m_{\alpha}=j(\pi_{\beta\alpha})(m_{\beta})
.
Since
m_{\beta}\leq j(q_{\beta\alpha})
,
\langle j(\mathbb{P}_{\beta}),m_{\beta},\tau_{\beta}\rangle\leq\langle j(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}),m_{\alpha},\tau_{\alpha}\rangle
. It follows that for all
\alpha<\beta<\gamma<\lambda
,
j(\pi_{\beta\alpha})\circ j(\pi_{\gamma\beta})=j(\pi_{\gamma\alpha})
below
m_{\gamma}
, whenever these are both defined.
Thus for each
\gamma<\lambda
,
\langle m_{\alpha}:\alpha<\gamma\rangle
is an element of

\varprojlim(\langle j(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}):\alpha<\gamma\rangle,\langle j(\pi_{\beta\alpha}):\alpha<\beta<\gamma\rangle)
, which can be computed in
M[A^{\prime}]
since
U\restriction\gamma\in M(\mathbb{R}_{1})
.

Let
\vec{\mathbb{P}}=\langle j(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}):\alpha<\lambda\rangle
and
\vec{\pi}=\langle j(\pi_{\beta\alpha}):\alpha<\beta<\lambda\rangle
.
We claim that for all
\gamma<\lambda
, the map

\sigma_{\gamma}:E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{P}},\vec{\pi}\rangle}_{\gamma}\to\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{P}}\restriction\gamma,\vec{\pi}\restriction\gamma),

defined by
p\mapsto\langle j(\pi_{\gamma\alpha})(p):\alpha<\gamma\rangle
, is a projection below
m_{\gamma}
.
To see this, let
\gamma<\lambda
and use the distributivity of
\mathbb{U}(G)
to find some
\xi<\lambda
such that
U\restriction(\gamma+1)\in V[A][G^{\tau_{\xi}}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}]
. In this model,
U\restriction(\gamma+1)
has the property that for all
x\in[\gamma+1]^{<\kappa}
with top element
\gamma
, if
\vec{\mathbb{Q}}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}:\alpha\in x\rangle
and
\vec{\chi}=\langle\pi_{\beta\alpha}:\{\alpha,\beta\}\in[x]^{2}\rangle
, then the map
\sigma_{x}:E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{Q}},\vec{\chi}\rangle}_{\gamma}\to\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{Q}},\vec{\chi})
defined by
p\mapsto\langle\pi_{\gamma\alpha}(p):\alpha\in x\cap\gamma\rangle
is a projection, below any condition that projects as in clause (
4
) of the definition of
\mathbb{U}(G)
.

If we force below
m_{\xi}
over
M[A^{\prime}]
, we get a lifted elementary embedding
j:V[A][G^{\tau_{\xi}}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}]\to M[A^{\prime}][K]
for some
K\subseteq j(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})
. By elementarity, the property holds of
U^{\prime}=j(U\restriction(\gamma+1))
in
M[A^{\prime}][K]
. In particular, it holds for
x=j[\gamma+1]
. By re-indexing
U^{\prime}\restriction j[\gamma+1]
by
\gamma+1
, we see that the desired claim holds in
M[A^{\prime}][K]
. Since
j(q)_{j(\beta),j(\alpha)}=j(q_{\beta\alpha})\geq m_{\beta}
for all
\{\alpha,\beta\}\in[\gamma+1]^{2}
,
\sigma_{\gamma}
is a projection below
m_{\gamma}
. But
U^{\prime}\restriction j[\gamma+1]\in M[A^{\prime}]
by the
\lambda
-closure of
j(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})
, so the desired claim already holds in
M[A^{\prime}]
.

To construct the sequence
\langle q_{\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda\rangle
, first enumerate all ordinals below
j(\lambda)
as
\langle\xi_{\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda\rangle
.
Let
\alpha_{0}
be such that it is forced that
j(\dom\tau_{\alpha_{0}})>\xi_{0}
. Let
p_{0}\in E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{P}},\vec{\pi}\rangle}_{\alpha_{0}}
be below
m_{\alpha_{0}}
and decide
j(\tau_{\alpha_{0}})(\xi_{0})
. Then let
\alpha_{1}\geq\alpha_{0}
be such that it is forced that
j(\dom\tau_{\alpha_{1}})>\xi_{1}
, and let
p_{1}\leq m_{\alpha_{1}}
be in
E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{P}},\vec{\pi}\rangle}_{\alpha_{1}}
and such that
j(\pi_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{0}})(p_{1})\leq p_{0}
and
p_{1}
decides
j(\tau_{\alpha_{1}})(\xi_{1})
. Continuing in this fashion, suppose we reach a limit
\zeta<\lambda
such that we have an increasing sequence of ordinals
\langle\alpha_{i}:i<\eta\rangle
and a sequence
\langle p_{i}:i<\eta\rangle
such that for
i<\eta
,

•

p_{i}\in E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{P}},\vec{\pi}\rangle}_{\alpha_{i}}
,

•

p_{i}
decides
j(\tau_{\alpha_{i}})(\xi_{i})
, and

•

\langle j(\pi_{\alpha_{k}\alpha_{i}})(p_{k}):i\leq k<\eta\rangle
is a descending sequence in
E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{P}},\vec{\pi}\rangle}_{\alpha_{i}}
below
m_{\alpha_{i}}
.

For
i<\eta
, let
p^{*}_{i}=\inf\{j(\pi_{\alpha_{k}\alpha_{i}})(p_{k}):i\leq k<\eta\}
. Let
\gamma=\sup_{i<\eta}\alpha_{i}
, and for
\beta<\gamma
, let
r_{\beta}=j(\pi_{\alpha_{i}\beta})(p_{i}^{*})
for any
i<\eta
such that
\alpha_{i}\geq\beta
. Then
\langle r_{\beta}:\beta<\gamma\rangle
is a member of
\varprojlim(\vec{\mathbb{P}}\restriction\gamma,\vec{\pi}\restriction\gamma)
below
\langle m_{\beta}:\beta<\gamma\rangle
. Thus we may select some
\alpha_{\eta}\geq\gamma
such that it is forced that
j(\dom\tau_{\alpha_{\eta}})>\xi_{\eta}
, and we may select some
p_{\eta}\in E^{\langle\vec{\mathbb{P}},\vec{\pi}\rangle}_{\alpha_{\eta}}
below
m_{\alpha_{\eta}}
such that
\langle j(\pi_{\alpha_{\eta}\beta})(p_{\alpha_{\eta}}):\beta<\gamma\rangle\leq\langle r_{\beta}:\beta<\gamma\rangle
and
p_{\alpha_{\eta}}
decides
j(\tau_{\alpha_{\eta}})(\xi_{\eta})
. Thus the induction can continue.

Put
q_{i}=\langle j(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}}),p_{i},j(\tau_{\alpha_{i}})\rangle
for
i<\lambda
. For each
p\in G
, there is some sufficiently large
i
and some
r\in G^{\tau_{\alpha_{i}}}_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}
such that
\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}},r,\tau_{\alpha_{i}}\rangle\leq p
, and so
q_{i}\leq\langle j(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}}),m_{\alpha_{i}},j(\tau_{\alpha_{i}})\rangle\leq j(p)
.
Recall the
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
-generic function
f:\lambda\to 2
from Lemma
28
, defined as
f(\beta)=n
iff there is
\langle\mathbb{P},p,\tau\rangle\in G
such that
p\Vdash\tau(\beta)=n
. For every
Y\subseteq\kappa
in
V[A{*}G]
, there is
\beta<\lambda
such that
Y=\{i<\kappa:f(\beta+i)=1\}
. If
\dot{x}_{\beta}
is a name for this set and
\tau
is any name such that
\tau^{G}=Y
, then there is
p\in G
such that
p\Vdash\tau=\dot{x}_{\beta}
.
If
i<\lambda
is sufficiently large, then
q_{i}\leq j(p)
and
q_{i}
decides
j(\tau_{\alpha_{i}})(j(\beta)+\kappa)
, meaning it decides whether
\kappa\in j(\tau)
. Therefore, we have our
V[A{*}G]
-normal ultrafilter
F
.

Let
I=\{Y\subseteq\kappa:1\Vdash_{\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\kappa)\times\mathbb{B}}Y\notin\dot{F}\}
. As in the proof of Theorem
44
,
I
is a normal ideal on
\kappa
, and the map
[Y]_{I}\mapsto\|\check{Y}\in\dot{F}\|
is a complete embedding from
\mathcal{P}(\kappa)/I
into
\mathcal{B}(\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\kappa)\times\mathbb{B})
. Since the latter has a dense set of size
\omega_{1}
,
I
is
\omega_{1}
-dense.
∎

Corollary 48
.

Suppose
\kappa_{1}<\kappa_{2}<\kappa_{3}
are such that for
n\in\{1,2\}
,
\kappa_{n}
is almost-huge with target
\kappa_{n+1}
. Then
\mathbb{A}(\omega,\kappa_{1})*\dot{\text{\char 87\relax}}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa_{2},{<}\kappa_{3})

forces that for
n\in\{1,2\}
, there is a normal ideal
I
on
\omega_{n}
such that

\mathcal{P}(\omega_{n})/I\cong\mathcal{B}(\mathrm{Col}(\omega_{n-1},\omega_{n})).

6
More dense ideals

Towards producing longer intervals of cardinals carrying dense ideals, we investigate the effect of iterating
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
. Recall that by Lemma
43
,
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\lambda)
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Col}(\kappa,\lambda)
.

Lemma 49
.

Suppose
\mu<\kappa<\lambda
are regular uncountable with
\kappa,\lambda
inaccessible. Let
\tau^{*}
be the
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
-name given by Lemma
43
. Then there is a projection
\psi
defined on a dense subset,

\psi:\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\text{\char 87\relax}}(\kappa,\lambda)^{\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})}\to\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\lambda)\restriction\langle\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa),1,\tau^{*}\rangle.

Proof.

To avoid confusion, we will refer to the tree forcing that is equivalent to the three-step iteration
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\mu,\kappa)
as
\mathbb{T}
.
We will define
\psi
on the dense set of conditions
\langle t,\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}},\dot{p},\dot{\tau}\rangle\rangle
such that for some cardinal
\delta\in(\kappa,\lambda)
,
\dot{\mathbb{P}}
is forced to have size
\delta
and collapse
\delta
to
\kappa
, and
\dot{\tau}
is forced to have domain
\delta
.
\psi
will map such a condition
\langle t,\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}},\dot{p},\dot{\tau}\rangle\rangle
to one of the form
\langle\mathbb{T}*\dot{\mathbb{P}},\langle t,\dot{p}\rangle,\sigma\rangle
.
To define
\sigma
, we interpret
\dot{\tau}
in
V^{\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathbb{P}}}
as coding a
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
-name in the following way. In
V
, fix some enumeration
\langle c_{\alpha}:\alpha<\kappa\rangle
of
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
. Let
f
be the interpretation of
\dot{\tau}
in a
(\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathbb{P}})
-generic extension. In this extension, for
\alpha<\delta
, let
A_{\alpha}=\{c_{\beta}:f(\kappa\cdot\alpha+\beta)=1\}
, and let
\sigma_{0}
be the
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
-name
\bigcup_{\alpha<\delta}A_{\alpha}\times\{\check{\alpha}\}
. Then let
\sigma_{1}
be a name for the characteristic function of
\sigma_{0}
.
In
V
, let
\sigma_{2}
be a
(\mathbb{T}{*}\dot{\mathbb{P}})
-name that is forced to evaluate to
\sigma_{1}^{K}
, where
K
is the
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
-generic on the third coordinate in the decomposition of
\mathbb{T}
given by Lemma
43
. Let
\sigma
be a name for the concatenation
\tau^{*}\,{}^{\frown}\sigma_{2}
.

\psi
is order-preserving because if
\langle t_{1},\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{1},\dot{p}_{1},\dot{\tau}_{1}\rangle\rangle\leq\langle t_{0},\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\dot{p}_{0},\dot{\tau}_{0}\rangle\rangle
are in the domain of
\psi
, then
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
forces that there is a
\kappa
-continuous projection from a dense
\kappa
-closed subset of
\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{1}
to
\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0}
. If
\dot{\pi}
is a name for such a projection such that
t_{1}
forces that
\dot{\pi}
witnesses the ordering of the second coordinates, then
\langle t,\dot{p}\rangle\mapsto\langle t,\dot{\pi}(\dot{p})\rangle
is a
\mu
-continuous projection from a dense subset of
\mathbb{T}*\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{1}
to
\mathbb{T}*\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0}
, and it clearly witnesses
\psi(\langle t_{1},\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{1},\dot{p}_{1},\dot{\tau}_{1}\rangle\rangle)\leq\psi(\langle t_{0},\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\dot{p}_{0},\dot{\tau}_{0}\rangle\rangle)
.
To show
\psi
is a projection, suppose

\langle\mathbb{Q},q,\dot{x}\rangle\leq\psi(t_{0},\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\dot{p}_{0},\dot{\tau}_{0}\rangle)=\langle\mathbb{T}*\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\langle c_{0},\dot{p}_{0}\rangle,\sigma_{0}\rangle.

We may assume that for some regular cardinal
\delta>|\mathbb{T}*\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0}|
,
\mathbb{Q}
has size
\delta
, it collapses
\delta
to
\mu
, and
\dot{x}
is forced to have length
\delta
. Let
\mathbb{Q}_{0}=\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{1}=\mathbb{T}*\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{1}=\mathbb{T}*(\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\times\dot{\mathrm{Col}}(\kappa,\delta)^{\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)})
. Let
\chi_{0}
be a projection witnessing the ordering
\langle\mathbb{Q},q,\dot{x}\rangle\leq\langle\mathbb{T}*\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\langle t_{0},\dot{p}_{0}\rangle,\sigma_{0}\rangle
, and let
\chi_{1}:\mathbb{Q}_{1}\to\mathbb{T}*\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0}
be
\langle a,\langle b,c\rangle\rangle\mapsto\langle a,b\rangle
.

By Lemma
12
, for
n<2
there is a dense
\mu
-closed
E_{n}\subseteq(\mathbb{T}*\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{0})\times\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\delta)
and a
\mu
-continuous dense embedding
e_{n}:E_{n}\to\mathbb{Q}_{n}
, with the property that if
\langle\langle a,b\rangle,c\rangle\in E_{n}
, then
\chi_{n}(e_{n}(\langle\langle a,b\rangle,c\rangle))=\langle a,b\rangle
.

Let
E=E_{0}\cap E_{1}
, and let
F_{n}=e_{n}[E]
. There is a
\mu
-continuous isomorphism
\iota:F_{1}\to F_{0}
defined by
\iota=e_{0}\circ e_{1}^{-1}
.
If
r\in F_{1}
and
r=e_{1}(a,b)
, then

\chi_{0}\circ\iota(r)=\chi_{0}\circ e_{0}(a,b)=a=\chi_{1}\circ e_{1}(a,b)=\chi_{1}(r).

If
\langle t_{1},\langle\dot{p}_{1},\dot{d}_{1}\rangle\rangle=r\in F_{1}
is such that
\iota(r)\leq q
, then since
q\Vdash\langle t_{0},\dot{p}_{0}\rangle\in\chi_{0}(\dot{G}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, we must have
\langle t_{1},\dot{p}_{1}\rangle=\chi_{1}(r)=\chi_{0}(\iota(r))\leq\langle t_{0},\dot{p}_{0}\rangle
.

Let
\delta_{0}
be the forced domain of
\dot{\tau}_{0}
. In the extension by
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})*\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{1}
, forcing with
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
yields a generic for
\mathbb{T}*\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{1}
, which then gives a
\mathbb{Q}
-generic
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
by projecting with
\iota
, with which we can evaluate
\dot{x}
. Let
\dot{y}
be a
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
-name that is forced to evaluate to
\dot{x}^{\dot{G}_{\mathbb{Q}}}
. For
\delta_{0}\leq\alpha<\delta
, let
A_{\alpha}\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
be the set of all conditions forcing
\dot{y}(\alpha)=1
. Then in the extension by
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})
, let
\dot{A}_{\alpha}
be a
\mathbb{P}_{1}
-name for such an
A_{\alpha}
, and let
\tau_{1}
be a name for a binary sequence of length
\delta
such that for
\delta_{0}\leq\alpha<\delta
, it is forced that for
\beta<\kappa
,
\tau_{1}(\kappa\cdot\alpha+\beta)=1
iff
c_{\beta}\in\dot{A}_{\alpha}
, where
\{c_{\beta}:\beta<\kappa\}
is the fixed enumeration of
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
from
V
. Let
\tau_{1}\restriction\delta_{0}
be forced to agree with
\tau_{0}
.

If
\langle t_{1},\langle\dot{p}_{1},\dot{d}_{1}\rangle\rangle
is as above, then by the definition of
\psi
,

\psi\left(t_{1},\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}}_{1},\langle\dot{p}_{1},\dot{d}_{1}\rangle,\dot{\tau}_{1}\rangle\right)\leq\langle\mathbb{Q},q,\dot{x}\rangle,

as witnessed by
\iota:F_{1}\to\mathbb{Q}
.
∎

Definition 50
.

A condition
\langle\mathbb{P},p,\tau\rangle\in\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
is called a
double coding condition
if for some regular cardinal
\delta
,

•

|\mathbb{P}|=\delta
, with enumeration
\{p_{\alpha}:\alpha<\delta\}
,

•

\mathbb{P}
collapses
\delta
to
\kappa
,

•

\tau
is forced to have length
\delta
, and

•

for some
\alpha<\delta
, it is forced that for all
\beta<\delta
and all
\gamma<\kappa
,

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}\tau(\alpha+\kappa\cdot\beta+\gamma)=1\leftrightarrow p_{\beta}\in\dot{G}.

In other words,
\dot{G}
is coded by some final segment of
\kappa
-blocks of 0’s and 1’s. It is a special type of strong coding that also occurs densely often in
\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)
.

Lemma 51
.

Suppose
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
forces that
\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}},\dot{p},\dot{\tau}\rangle\in\text{\char 87\relax}(\kappa,\lambda)^{\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})}
is a double coding condition with witnessing cardinal
\delta
. Then for all
c\in\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
,
\psi(c,\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}},\dot{p},\dot{\tau}\rangle)
is a coding condition in
\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\lambda)
.

Proof.

Let
\psi(c,\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}},\dot{p},\dot{\tau}\rangle)=\langle\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{P}},\langle c,\dot{p}\rangle,\sigma\rangle
. Let
\dot{G}_{0},\dot{G}_{1}
be names for the generic filters over the first and second coordinates of
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{P}}
respectively. By the way
\sigma
is constructed from
\tau
, it is forced by
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{P}}
that there is
\alpha<\delta
such that for all
\beta<\delta
,
\sigma(\alpha+\beta)=1
iff
\dot{p}_{\beta}\in\dot{G}_{1}
. Since
\sigma
is forced to end-extend
\tau^{*}
, it is forced that
\dot{G}_{0}
can be read off from
\sigma\restriction\kappa\cdot 2
. Thus if
G_{0}*G_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{P}}
is generic, and
W
is any further generic extension, then any
V
-generic
H_{0}*H_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{P}}
that differs from
G_{0}*G_{1}
would evaluate
\sigma
differently.
∎

Lemma 52
.

The quotient forcing of the projection
\psi
of Lemma
49
is forced to be
\lambda
-distributive.

Proof.

Suppose
B*H\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\text{\char 87\relax}}(\kappa,\lambda)^{\text{\char 87\relax}(\mu,\kappa)*\dot{\mathbb{U}}(\dot{G})}
is generic. Let
G^{\prime}=\psi(B{*}H)
, and let
G*U*K
be the generic corresponding to
B
per Lemma
43
. For any set of ordinals
x\in V[B{*}H]
of size
<\lambda
, there is a
\mathrm{Col}(\mu,\kappa)
-name
\dot{x}\in V[G{*}U{*}H]
such that
\dot{x}^{K}=x
, and
\dot{x}\in V[G{*}U{*}H^{\tau}_{\mathbb{P}}]
for some double coding condition
\langle\mathbb{P},1,\tau\rangle\in H
, so
x\in V[B{*}H^{\tau}_{\mathbb{P}}]
. We can take names
\langle\dot{\mathbb{P}},\dot{1},\dot{\tau}\rangle
that are forced to be double coding with witness
\delta
, such that
